## misc/gnopreview/comment.go:24 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/comment.go#L24) · Warning
State: CONFIRMED, band: Warning, angle: catalog
TL;DR: a push that reverts the last realm change leaves the previous comment standing, so the PR shows screenshots and links of an older head
Check: cd misc/gnopreview && go test -run TestCatalogEmptyPlanCanClearTheComment -v . ; grep -n "skip != " .github/workflows/pr-preview.yml ; grep -n "continue-on-error\|outcome == \|no comment body" .github/workflows/pr-preview-publish.yml
Details: Confirmed by rerunning the artifact and by reading all three workflows. Comment returns "" for a plan with nothing to preview (comment.go:24-26), and no path on the publish side ever removes or rewrites the comment for that case: pr-preview.yml gates the gnodev build, the render and actions/upload-artifact on `if: steps.plan.outputs.skip != 'true'` (lines 85, 92, 118), so no artifact exists; pr-preview-publish.yml downloads with `continue-on-error: true` (54) and gates every later step on `steps.download.outcome == 'success'` (68), so the job is green and silent; and the upsert step exits 0 on `[ -s _preview/comment.md ]` (166) before reaching the only two writes, the PATCH at 174 and the POST at 177. The one deletion is pr-preview-cleanup.yml on `pull_request_target: closed`, so the stale body survives every further push until the PR closes. What the reader loses is the signal, not the preview: the comment keeps saying "Rebuilt on every push to this PR" while linking snapshots of a head that no longer exists. Fix: make Comment emit the marker and a one-line "nothing to preview on this head" body for an empty plan, and let the skip branch upload that artifact so the upsert replaces the old comment.
Evidence: Head ecf7af0f2. Finder's artifact rerun from the file: `go test -run TestCatalogEmptyPlanCanClearTheComment -v .` — `zz_b5_catalog_test.go:32: Comment(empty plan) = ""; a body carrying "<!-- gnoweb-pr-preview -->" is the only way to replace the previous push comment` — FAIL. Workflow lines read at this head: pr-preview.yml 85/92/118 `if: steps.plan.outputs.skip != 'true'`; pr-preview-publish.yml 54 `continue-on-error: true`, 68 `if: steps.download.outcome == 'success'`, 166 `[ -s _preview/comment.md ] || { echo "no comment body — nothing to post"; exit 0; }`, 174 `gh api -X PATCH ... -F body=@_preview/comment.md`, 177 `gh api ... -F body=@_preview/comment.md`.
Artifact: reviews/pr/6xxx/6194-gnoweb-pr-preview/1-ecf7af0/tests/b5-catalog-stale-sticky-comment_test.go

## misc/gnopreview/comment.go:62 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/comment.go#L62) · Warning
State: CONFIRMED, band: Warning, angle: lines + catalog + reach
TL;DR: changed realms the cap dropped are posted as links to pages the preview never rendered
Check: cp tests/b10-lines-cap-drops-changed-realms_test.go misc/gnopreview/b10_cap_test.go && cd misc/gnopreview && go test -run TestB10CapDropsChangedRealms -v ./... | cd misc/gnopreview && go test -run TestCatalogChangedRealmsSurviveTheCap -v . | cd misc/gnopreview && go test -run TestCommentLinksUnrenderedChangedRealms (artifact below): BuildPlan with three changed realms and maxRealms=2, then check each ChangedRealm absent from Realms against the comment text | cp the artifact into misc/gnopreview and run `go test -run TestReachB5 -v ./...`: TestReachB5TreeWideChangeOutrunsTheCap feeds every examples/gno.land/r gnomod.toml as changed at the default cap, TestReachB5CommentLinksUnrenderedChangedRealms does it on the fake repo at cap 1
Details: plan.go:256-259 truncates the combined realm list at maxRealms and leaves Plan.ChangedRealms untouched; the stable sort above it only guarantees changed realms come first, which stops helping once there are more of them than the cap. comment.go:60-64 then emits one link per ChangedRealm with source and help tabs, so every changed realm past the cap gets a https://<pages>/r/.../ URL the crawler never wrote. examples/gno.land/r holds 58 realm directories against a default cap of 25, so one gnomod.toml or gno fmt sweep is enough. The reviewer clicks a link the comment presents exactly like the working ones and lands on a 404, with nothing saying which of the 58 are real. Fix: build the changed-realm list from the intersection with Plan.Realms, or give the cap a floor that never truncates below len(ChangedRealms).
Evidence: judge-67 on the real tree, every examples/gno.land/r gnomod.toml as changed at the default cap: 'changed files=58 ChangedRealms=58 Realms=25 Dropped=33 cap=25' then '33 changed realm(s) linked but never rendered, e.g. https://example.test/pr-1/r/gov/dao/treasury/test/v0/ for gno.land/r/gov/dao/treasury/test/v0'. The finder's TestCommentLinksUnrenderedChangedRealms reruns FAIL at cap 2 with 'ChangedRealms=[gno.land/r/x/a1 gno.land/r/x/a2 gno.land/r/x/a3] Realms=[gno.land/r/x/a1 gno.land/r/x/a2] Dropped=1'.
Artifact: reviews/pr/6xxx/6194-gnoweb-pr-preview/1-ecf7af0/tests/judge-67-cap-drops-changed-realms_test.go

## misc/gnopreview/comment.go:69 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/comment.go#L69) · Warning
State: CONFIRMED, band: Warning, angle: claims + catalog + lines + reach
TL;DR: a realm the changed package reaches is dropped from the comment whenever the same PR also touches gnoweb
Check: read comment.go:56-82 with isSeed at 157-162: the only listings in the default branch are ChangedRealms and indirect, and indirect excludes every member of gnowebSeedRealms when p.Gnoweb | go test -run TestClaimAffectedSeedRealmIsUnlisted: Comment(&Plan{Gnoweb:true, ChangedPkgs:["gno.land/p/x/base/v0"], Realms:["gno.land/r/gnoland/home","gno.land/r/x/other"]}, base, "1") must mention gno.land/r/gnoland/home and say (2); observe the realm absent and (1) | Comment(&Plan{Gnoweb: true, ChangedRealms: []string{"gno.land/r/x/leaf"}, Realms: []string{"gno.land/r/gnoland/home", "gno.land/r/x/leaf"}, Pairs: []ShotPair{{Realm: "gno.land/r/x/leaf", After: "_shots/a.png", URL: "r/x/leaf/"}}}, base, pr) and grep the output for r/gnoland/home | cd misc/gnopreview && go test -run TestCommentDropsAffectedSeedRealm (artifact below): BuildPlan on a fixture where r/gnoland/home imports the changed p/x/base/v0 plus a gnoweb file yields Mode=both, Realms=[gno.land/r/gnoland/home], and Comment() output contains no /r/gnoland/home link | `go test -run TestReachB5CommentHidesIndirectSeedRealm -v ./...` in misc/gnopreview with the artifact copied in: it builds the plan from the real tree and asserts every rendered realm is named in the comment
Details: isSeed() at comment.go:157 returns true for any of the four gnowebSeedRealms as soon as Plan.Gnoweb is set, and the indirect loop at :68 skips it, so membership in the seed list, not the reason the realm is in Plan.Realms, decides whether it is listed. BuildPlan puts gno.land/r/gnoland/home in Plan.Realms because it imports the changed gno.land/p/moul/dynreplacer/v0 (plan.go:236-247), gnodev renders it and Index lists it, and the comment names no realm but the changed one. Shots does not cover the gap: main.go:148-152 fills plan.Shots only when len(plan.ChangedRealms)==0, so a PR with a changed realm alongside gets an empty shotGrid. The reviewer is told the PR affects realm sources and is never shown the realm the change actually reaches. Fix: decide the skip on why the realm is in Plan.Realms, skipping a seed only when nothing changed reaches it, instead of on membership in gnowebSeedRealms.
Evidence: judge-66 on the real examples/ tree, changed = gno.land/pkg/gnoweb/app.go + examples/gno.land/p/moul/dynreplacer/v0/gnomod.toml + examples/gno.land/r/demo/counter/gnomod.toml: 'Mode=both Gnoweb=true ChangedRealms=[gno.land/r/demo/counter] ChangedPkgs=[gno.land/p/moul/dynreplacer/v0] Realms=[gno.land/r/demo/counter gno.land/r/docs/security_patterns gno.land/r/gnoland/blog gno.land/r/gnoland/boards2/v0 gno.land/r/gnoland/home]' and the emitted body carries only '- [`gno.land/r/demo/counter`](https://example.test/pr-1/r/demo/counter/)'. Differential in the same tree: drop the gnoweb file and keep the identical package change and TestProbeNoGnoweb logs 'Gnoweb=false Mode=realms Realms=[gno.land/r/gnoland/home]' with 'comment mentions home=true'. The Gnoweb flag alone is what hides the realm. The finder's own TestCommentDropsAffectedSeedRealm reruns FAIL with the same shape.
Artifact: reviews/pr/6xxx/6194-gnoweb-pr-preview/1-ecf7af0/tests/judge-66-seed-realm-affected-by-a-changed-package_test.go

## misc/gnopreview/comment.go:86 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/comment.go#L86) · Warning
State: CONFIRMED, band: Warning, angle: catalog + claims + lines
TL;DR: the cap note tells the reviewer the changed realms were kept in the run where the cap dropped them
Check: cd misc/gnopreview && go test -count=1 -v -run TestCapDropsChangedRealmsWhileCommentDeniesIt (fixture: 30 changed realms, defaultMaxRealms); assert every ChangedRealms entry appears in Realms and that no dropped realm's URL appears in Comment() | go test -run TestClaimChangedRealmsAreDropped: BuildPlan with three changed realms and maxRealms=1, then Comment on the result — no ChangedRealms entry may be missing from Realms while the body carries "the changed realms are always kept" | go test ./misc/gnopreview -run TestCapDropsChangedRealms with 2 changed realms and maxRealms=1: expect every ChangedRealms entry in Realms, observe one dropped and still linked | cp reviews/pr/6xxx/6194-gnoweb-pr-preview/1-ecf7af0/tests/b5-claims-comment_test.go misc/gnopreview/zz_b5_claims_test.go && cd misc/gnopreview && go test ./... -run TestB5DroppedChangedRealmAgainstTheNote -v
Details: The note at comment.go:86 states 'the changed realms are always kept' unconditionally, and plan.go:253-259 does not keep them: the stable sort puts changed realms first, then the truncation cuts the whole slice at maxRealms with no floor, so a diff with more changed realms than the cap loses the surplus. The note is the only thing the reviewer has to tell a missing realm from a rendered one, and it says the opposite of what happened, so it reads the dropped realms as indirect dependents that were not worth rendering. Measured on the real tree, 33 of 58 changed realms were dropped under that sentence. Fix: derive the note from the run, naming how many of the dropped realms were changed, or hold the invariant by flooring the cap at len(ChangedRealms).
Evidence: judge-67 TestJudge67CapNoteContradictsTheDrop on every examples/gno.land/r gnomod.toml at the default cap: 'changed realms dropped by the cap=33 Dropped=33' then 'cap dropped 33 of 58 changed realms, yet the comment says "the changed realms are always kept"'. The finder's TestB5DroppedChangedRealmAgainstTheNote reruns FAIL at cap 2 with the dropped realm's link sitting above the note in the same body.
Artifact: reviews/pr/6xxx/6194-gnoweb-pr-preview/1-ecf7af0/tests/judge-67-cap-drops-changed-realms_test.go

## misc/gnopreview/comment.go:106 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/comment.go#L106) · Warning
State: CONFIRMED, band: Warning, angle: lines
TL;DR: a merge-base render that failed leaves the comment showing one unlabelled screenshot and no word that there is no baseline
Check: go test ./... -run 'B5Baseline|B5NewRealm' -v in misc/gnopreview at ecf7af0f2 with the artifact test: expect the comment to name the missing baseline, observe a body byte-identical to the New-realm body minus its <sub> note
Details: renderBase returns a nil *Crawler on every failure path — startGnodev error, waitReady timeout, base.Run error, base.Write error, and an empty -base-root — each reporting only to stderr, while the newRealms map it returns marks only realms absent from cfg.baseRoot. A realm that does exist at the merge base therefore arrives at ScreenshotPairs with base == nil, so the shots.go:88 'if base != nil' guard is skipped, Before stays empty and New stays false. pairGrid's 'if p.Before == ""' branch then emits the after image with note == "", so the reader gets the realm name, one picture, and nothing else; the two-column 'before — merge base' / 'after — this PR' table that is the tool's whole product is gone with no explanation. The code already states this invariant for the New flag at shots.go:39-43 ('claiming a realm is new when we simply did not look would be a lie in the comment') and does not keep it for the silent case. Fix: carry the baseline outcome on ShotPair and have pairGrid say 'baseline unavailable — the merge-base render failed' when Before is empty and New is false.
Evidence: Finder's fixture rerun from the file as zz_b5_test.go in the judge scratch worktree at ecf7af0f2: both tests PASS. The failed-baseline body it printed, verbatim: '**`gno.land/r/x/leaf`**' then '<a href="https://example.test/pr-1/r/x/leaf/"><img src="https://example.test/pr-1/_shots/a.png" width="600" alt="gno.land/r/x/leaf"></a>' then '**Changed realms (1)**' — no 'before', 'merge base', 'baseline', 'compare' or 'unavailable' anywhere, and the closing <sub> speaks only of the static snapshot. Read chain confirming the fixture is the state main.go leaves: main.go:246/252/267/271/275 return nil with newRealms, shots.go:88 gates the before shot on base != nil, comment.go:106-114 renders the lone image with note empty unless p.New.
Artifact: reviews/pr/6xxx/6194-gnoweb-pr-preview/1-ecf7af0/tests/b5-lines-r2-baseline-unannounced.go

## misc/gnopreview/comment.go:112 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/comment.go#L112) · Warning
State: CONFIRMED, band: Warning, angle: claims
TL;DR: a fork's realm directory name reaches the bot comment unescaped, so it can introduce markup into a comment the repository's own token posts
Check: cp the artifact and run `go test ./... -run TestB5CommentEscapesRealmNames -v` in misc/gnopreview; then settle the remaining step end to end — boot gnodev on a package dir whose name carries `"` or `)` and see whether it serves, since the render job writes no comment.md if gnodev refuses to boot on plan.Dirs.
Details: Confirmed by read and by my own run. Realm paths are the fork's directory names: plan.go:136-141 sets Pkg.Path from the walked directory and never reads the gnomod module line, so nothing validates them. comment.go then writes that string raw into markdown code spans (33, 63, 80, 154) and into HTML attributes (112, 118, 121), and pr-preview-publish.yml:174/177 posts the resulting file verbatim as github-actions[bot] under the repository token. Two limits I measured that the candidate does not state. First, the finder's own payload is not deliverable: pr-preview.yml's `git diff --name-only` is the only source of the changed-file list, and git quotes a path containing `"` (`"examples/gno.land/r/ev\"il/lib.gno"`), so the alt= breakout it demonstrated never reaches BuildPlan; a backtick is passed through unquoted and closes the code span instead, which is the deliverable form. Second, the render must still finish: plan.Dirs hands the hostile directory to gnodev, and waitReady (crawl.go:589) needs HTTP 200 on Realms[0], so the attack wants a second, ordinary realm sorting first — a link I read but did not boot. Fix: escape every realm and package path before it is written, `html.EscapeString` for the attribute forms and a backtick-and-bracket escape for the markdown ones, or drop any path outside `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]+` from the comment.
Evidence: Head ecf7af0f2, misc/gnopreview. Finder's artifact rerun from the file: `go test -run TestB5CommentEscapesRealmNames -v .` FAILs with `alt="x"><img src="https://attacker.example/pixel.png">">`. My own check, tests/judge-83-realm-name-deliverable-by-git_test.go: TestJudge83QuoteIsNotDeliverable PASSes (git reports [] — the quoted path is dropped), TestJudge83BacktickBreaksTheCodeSpan FAILs with ChangedRealms=["gno.land/r/aaa" "gno.land/r/zz`<img src=x onerror=1>`y"] and the emitted line `- [`gno.land/r/zz`<img src=x onerror=1>`y`](https://example.test/pr-1/r/zz`<img src=x onerror=1>`y/)`. gnomod/file.go:122 `module.CheckImportPath` rejects such a module path, which is why the payload rides the directory name.
Artifact: reviews/pr/6xxx/6194-gnoweb-pr-preview/1-ecf7af0/tests/judge-83-realm-name-deliverable-by-git_test.go

## misc/gnopreview/crawl.go:40 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/crawl.go#L40) · Warning
State: CONFIRMED, band: Warning, angle: lines
TL;DR: the crawl-time gnodev host reaches the file on disk, not just the in-memory rewrite
Check: go test -run TestSnapshotKeepsCrawlTimeOrigin ./... in misc/gnopreview with tests/b2-lines-r2-help-origin_test.go: crawl a $help body shaped like action.html against an httptest server, write the snapshot, assert the server host does not survive
Details: This candidate's check goes further than #190's: it runs the whole pipeline, Run() then Write(), and reads the written index.html. The crawl-time host is still in the file, so the defect is in the published artifact and not an artefact of calling rewrite() directly. attrRe at crawl.go:40 is the single cause, and mapURL would not help either, crawl.go:382 returns raw untouched for anything not starting with a single "/". Same user loss as #190, seen one layer later. Fix: same one place, strip the crawl origin before mapURL and cover action= and data-copy-text-value=.
Evidence: Rerunning the finder's artifact from the file, misc/gnopreview, go test -run TestSnapshotKeepsCrawlTimeOrigin -v ./...: '--- FAIL: TestSnapshotKeepsCrawlTimeOrigin', 'published snapshot still points at the crawl-time gnodev 127.0.0.1:38995', written page holding '<form class="params" method="GET" action="http://127.0.0.1:38995/r/x/y$help&amp;func=Foo">' and '<button ... data-copy-text-value="http://127.0.0.1:38995/r/x/y$help&func=Foo">'; the sibling href on the same page did become ../../../../../r/x/y/_t/source/. The port differs from the finder's log (38995 here, 43095 there) because httptest picks it, which is what shows the run is mine. Independently: crawl.go:92 add(u + "$help") seeds one per realm, so no preview escapes it.
Artifact: reviews/pr/6xxx/6194-gnoweb-pr-preview/1-ecf7af0/tests/b2-lines-r2-help-origin_test.go

## misc/gnopreview/crawl.go:354 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/crawl.go#L354) · Warning
State: CONFIRMED, band: Warning, angle: reach
TL;DR: every published $help page keeps the crawler's own 127.0.0.1 origin in its form action and its copy-link button
Check: go test ./... -run TestHelpPageKeepsCrawlerOrigin -v in misc/gnopreview with the artifact test copied in: feed rewrite() the two attribute shapes action.html emits with the crawl origin expanded and count the surviving occurrences of http://127.0.0.1:8899; the sibling test pins that href= in the same body IS rewritten, so a failure is the attribute set and not the fixture
Details: Rendering the real HelpView (not a fixture) with Origin="http://127.0.0.1:8899" emits exactly two attributes carrying that origin, action= on the params form and data-copy-text-value= on the Link button, and neither is href or src. rewrite() at crawl.go:354 walks attrRe (crawl.go:40) which matches only href|src, so both survive verbatim; Seeds() adds u+"$help" for every realm at crawl.go:92, so every previewed realm ships one. A reviewer who presses Link on a published preview copies a loopback URL, and submitting the args form navigates to a refused connection. README.md:99 states "Every absolute URL is rewritten", which is false for these two. Fix: strip a leading c.Base (or any scheme+host) from the value before mapURL, and widen attrRe to the attributes gnoweb builds from Origin (action, data-copy-text-value).

## misc/gnopreview/main.go:199 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/main.go#L199) · Warning
State: CONFIRMED, band: Warning, angle: claims + lines
TL;DR: a gnodev that dies mid-crawl is invisible: Run() returns nil, render() publishes a partial snapshot and the job is green
Check: go test -run ClaimsR2 -v ./misc/gnopreview with tests/b3-claims-r2-node-death-mid-crawl.go copied to misc/gnopreview/: the server answers the first seed then closes its listener; expect Run() to report the dead node, observe err=<nil> | tests/b3-lines-r2-gnodev-orphan.sh: start the built gnopreview under setsid with a stub gnodev, send SIGINT to its process group, count survivors
Details: The died channel is read only inside waitReady's select (crawl.go, waitReady loop), which stops once the probe answers; after main.go:131 nothing reads it. A gnodev that dies during the crawl therefore reaches nobody: Crawler.Run prints '! <url>: connection refused' per URL at crawl.go:129-131 and continues, and returns nil, so render() goes on to Write, Index, Screenshot and Comment and exits 0. The reviewer gets a snapshot missing most of its realms with a sticky comment linking every realm in the plan, including the ones that have no page, and nothing in the job says so. Separately, gnodev is started with SysProcAttr{Setpgid: true} at main.go:190 and the module imports os/signal nowhere, so a Ctrl-C reaches gnopreview alone, its `defer stop()` never runs, and gnodev survives holding -port and -port+10000. Fix: keep reading died past readiness and fail the render on it, and install a signal handler that runs stop().
Evidence: Reran the finder's fixture from the file at ecf7af0, go test -run ClaimsR2 -v . in misc/gnopreview: '✓ /r/demo/a', then '! /r/demo/b: ... connect: connection refused' and the same for /r/demo/c, 'seeds=3 captured=1 err=<nil>', 'DEFECT: Run() == nil after the node died; 1 of 3 realm pages captured'. Read for the orphan half: main.go:190 `cmd.SysProcAttr = &syscall.SysProcAttr{Setpgid: true}`, and grep -rn 'os/signal|signal.Notify|NotifyContext' over misc/gnopreview returns nothing. Merge base has no misc/gnopreview, so both are the diff's.
Artifact: reviews/pr/6xxx/6194-gnoweb-pr-preview/1-ecf7af0/tests/b3-claims-r2-node-death-mid-crawl.go

## misc/gnopreview/shots.go:166 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/shots.go#L166) · Warning
State: CONFIRMED, band: Warning, angle: reach
TL;DR: one external image in a fork's realm markdown holds the preview job open for the runner's full six hours
Check: cp reviews/.../tests/b4-reach-chromeshot-hang_test.go misc/gnopreview/ && cd misc/gnopreview && go test -run TestChromeShot -v -timeout 5m .  — control returns in <1s, the stalled-subresource case is still running at 60s
Details: A realm page the PR changed is screenshotted by ScreenshotPairs, and its markdown reaches the snapshot unfiltered: AllowSvgDataImage (ext_imgvalidator.go:31-34) returns true for every URI that is not a data: URI, and Crawler.mapURL (crawl.go) returns raw untouched unless it starts with a single '/', so `![](http://host/x.png)` survives into the captured HTML. Chrome then opens that connection; --virtual-time-budget=4000 stops the page's virtual clock but does not bound the process, and chromeShot's exec.Command has no context, so the render step never returns. pr-preview.yml runs on pull_request from any fork and sets no timeout-minutes. Fix: use exec.CommandContext with a deadline in chromeShot, and set timeout-minutes on the preview job.
Evidence: My run at ecf7af0f2, real browser on this host: TestChromeShotPlainPage 'control returned in 418.263203ms' PASS; TestChromeShotStalledSubresource FAIL (60.01s) 'chromeShot still running after 1m0s'. pgrep afterwards shows the live child: /opt/google/chrome/chrome --headless=new ... --virtual-time-budget=4000 --screenshot=/tmp/TestChromeShotStalledSubresource.../shot.png http://127.0.0.1:46559/index.html. Reach read: ext_imgvalidator.go:34 'return !strings.HasPrefix(lower, "data:") || strings.HasPrefix(lower, svgdata)'; mapURL's first line returns raw for anything not '/'-prefixed. Merge base ships none of this code.
Artifact: reviews/pr/6xxx/6194-gnoweb-pr-preview/1-ecf7af0/tests/b4-reach-chromeshot-hang_test.go

## misc/gnopreview/shots.go:171 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/shots.go#L171) · Warning
State: CONFIRMED, band: Warning, angle: catalog
TL;DR: a browser that never exits pins the preview runner for six hours and the PR gets no comment
Check: go test -run TestChromeShotHasNoDeadline in misc/gnopreview with a stub browser that sleeps: chromeShot must return a deadline error; observe it still blocked after 6s. Plus grep -c timeout-minutes .github/workflows/pr-preview*.yml, all three 0.
Details: chromeShot builds exec.Command with no context (shots.go:171-183) and blocks in CombinedOutput at shots.go:184; nothing in the call chain cancels it. cfg.timeout (main.go:76) is passed only to waitReady (main.go:131, main.go:267), so it bounds gnodev readiness and not the browser. All three preview workflows carry zero timeout-minutes, so a stuck Chrome holds the job to the runner's 360-minute default with no preview posted and no log, since the render step's cleanup never runs. Fix: build the command with exec.CommandContext under a per-shot deadline (a minute is generous against the 0.4s a real page takes) and add timeout-minutes to pr-preview.yml.
Evidence: My run at ecf7af0f2 in a scratch worktree: TestChromeShotHasNoDeadline --- PASS (6.00s), logging 'chromeShot still blocked after 6s on a browser that never exits: no deadline bounds exec.Command' against a /bin/sh stub that only sleeps. grep -c timeout-minutes over pr-preview.yml, pr-preview-publish.yml, pr-preview-cleanup.yml: 0, 0, 0. I also saw a real /opt/google/chrome/chrome still alive past 60s on a page with one stalled subresource, so the hang is not only theoretical. Merge base has no misc/gnopreview and no pr-preview workflows.
Artifact: reviews/pr/6xxx/6194-gnoweb-pr-preview/1-ecf7af0/tests/b4-catalog-chromeshot-timeout_test.go

## misc/gnopreview/crawl.go:370 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/crawl.go#L370) · Missing test
State: CONFIRMED, band: Missing test, angle: claims
TL;DR: no test ties robotsRe to gnoweb's own head.html, so a reformat of that tag silently ships both robots directives on every preview page
Check: in a scratch worktree rewrite head.html:38 to `<meta content="index, follow" name="robots" />`, then `cd misc/gnopreview && go test ./...` and `cd gno.land && go test ./pkg/gnoweb/...`: both stay green while robotsRe no longer matches the page gnoweb serves. The test that would fail does not exist: a case reading ../../gno.land/pkg/gnoweb/components/layouts/head.html from disk and asserting robotsRe.MatchString on it.
Details: robotsRe at crawl.go:39 is `(?i)<meta\s+name="robots"[^>]*>` and gnoweb's tag at head.html:38 is `<meta name="robots" content="index, follow" />`, so the pair matches today by coincidence of spelling alone. Nothing holds it together: grep over gno.land/pkg/gnoweb returns no hit for robots in any _test.go, and the only copy of that tag inside misc/gnopreview is a literal re-typed at crawl_test.go:178. Reorder the attributes or switch the quotes and setNoindex falls through to its headRe arm, so every published preview page carries `index, follow` and `noindex, nofollow` at once and each crawler picks a winner. Fix: add a case to crawl_test.go that os.ReadFile's ../../gno.land/pkg/gnoweb/components/layouts/head.html and asserts robotsRe.MatchString on it; the separate go.mod blocks an import, not a disk read.
Evidence: crawl.go:39 robotsRe = regexp.MustCompile(`(?i)<meta\s+name="robots"[^>]*>`); gno.land/pkg/gnoweb/components/layouts/head.html:38 `<meta name="robots" content="index, follow" />`; grep -rn robots over gno.land/pkg/gnoweb filtered to _test.go -> no hit; grep -rn head.html over misc/gnopreview -> README.md:124 only, prose. misc/gnopreview/go.mod line 1: module github.com/gnolang/gno/misc/gnopreview.

Check: in a scratch worktree at ecf7af0: `sed -i 's|up := strings.Repeat("../", depth)|up := strings.Repeat("../", depth+1)|' misc/gnopreview/crawl.go && cd misc/gnopreview && go test ./...` — expect a failure, observe `ok`

My own TestJudge153RewriteResolvesFromThePageDir passes at the unmutated head and goes red on the mutation with `page r/x/y/index.html: "/r/a/b" rewritten to "../../../../r/a/b/", which resolves to "../r/a/b"; want "r/a/b" — the published link 404s`, and again for /public/css/main.css and for the deeper r/x/y/_t/source/index.html. ## misc/gnopreview/crawl_test.go:180 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/crawl_test.go#L180) · Missing test
State: CONFIRMED, band: Missing test, angle: tests
TL;DR: the robots-meta test counts the tag but never says where it landed, so the <head> insertion can die and every preview page ships in quirks mode
Check: `sed -i 's|if loc := headRe.FindStringIndex(body); loc != nil {|if loc := headRe.FindStringIndex(body); false \&\& loc != nil {|' misc/gnopreview/crawl.go && cd misc/gnopreview && go test -run TestRewriteAddsNoindex ./...` — expect a failure, observe `ok`
Details: setNoindex has three branches (crawl.go:371-378) and the last is `return noindexTag + body`. TestRewriteAddsNoindex's four assertions check presence, a count of 1, a single robots meta and the absence of "index, follow" — the fallback satisfies all four, for the two cases (`normal head`, `head with attrs`) that exist to exercise the headRe insertion. With the head branch dead, the tag is prepended ahead of `<!doctype html>`, and content before the doctype puts the page into quirks mode: the preview a reviewer is asked to judge gnoweb's CSS by would box differently than gno.land. Fix: assert the tag's index falls between `<head` and `</head>`, and that no byte precedes the doctype.
Evidence: Head ecf7af0f, scratch worktree. Mutation `if loc := headRe.FindStringIndex(body); false && loc != nil {` at crawl.go:374 -> `go test -vet=off -count=1 -run TestRewriteAddsNoindex .` = ok 0.003s, and the full package also ok 0.004s. My own TestJudge154NoindexLandsInsideHead passes unmutated and goes red on it: `normal head: robots meta at 0 is outside <head> (69..91)` and `normal head: 48 bytes precede the doctype: <meta name="robots" content="noindex, nofollow"><!doctype ht`, plus `head with attrs: robots meta at 0 is outside <head> (54..86)`. The merge base carries no misc/gnopreview.

## misc/gnopreview/plan_test.go:133 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/plan_test.go#L133) · Missing test
State: CONFIRMED, band: Missing test, angle: tests
TL;DR: the table's own defaulting makes it impossible for a case to pass the documented no-cap value to BuildPlan
Check: sed -i 's#if maxRealms > 0 && len(realms) > maxRealms {#if maxRealms >= 0 \&\& len(realms) > maxRealms {#' plan.go && go test ./... ; expect a failure, observe ok. The fix is a `*int` or a -1 sentinel in the table so 0 reaches BuildPlan.
Details: The subtest maps a zero tc.maxRealms to defaultMaxRealms at plan_test.go:132-135, so every one of the eleven cases calls BuildPlan with a positive limit and 0 never crosses the boundary. -max-realms is documented at main.go:73 as `cap on rendered realms; 0 for no cap` and implemented as the `maxRealms > 0` guard at plan.go:256, so the mode the flag documents is the one mode the table has designed itself out of reaching. Flip that comparison and -max-realms 0 truncates Realms to empty, turning every uncapped run into a preview of nothing with no error, and the suite stays green. Fix: make the table field a *int, or use -1 as the use-the-default sentinel, so a case can pass 0 through.
Evidence: plan_test.go:132-135 `limit := tc.maxRealms; if limit == 0 { limit = defaultMaxRealms }; got, err := BuildPlan(root, tc.changed, limit)`. plan.go:256 `if maxRealms > 0 && len(realms) > maxRealms {`. main.go:73 fs.IntVar(&cfg.maxRealms, "max-realms", defaultMaxRealms, "cap on rendered realms; 0 for no cap").
Artifact: reviews/pr/6xxx/6194-gnoweb-pr-preview/1-ecf7af0/tests/b10-tests-r2-mutation-green.sh

## misc/gnopreview/plan_test.go:135 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/plan_test.go#L135) · Missing test
State: CONFIRMED, band: Missing test, angle: reach
TL;DR: no call to BuildPlan anywhere in the suite passes 0, so the flag value documented as no cap is reached by nothing
Check: in the head worktree change plan.go:256 to `maxRealms >= 0 &&` and run go test ./misc/gnopreview: expect red, observe green
Details: plan_test.go:136 is the only BuildPlan call site in the package, and the two lines above it rewrite a zero limit into defaultMaxRealms, so the argument is always positive. The guard that gives 0 its documented meaning, plan.go:256 `maxRealms > 0`, therefore has one of its two outcomes unreachable from the tests: no case distinguishes `maxRealms > 0` from `maxRealms >= 0`, and under the latter -max-realms 0 slices Realms to realms[:0] and a realm PR silently reports nothing to preview. This is the same absence candidate 239 names from the mutation angle; one fixture settles both. Fix: pass the sentinel through — a *int field or -1 for use-the-default — and add a case asserting 0 leaves every affected realm in Realms.
Evidence: grep for BuildPlan( in misc/gnopreview/plan_test.go -> plan_test.go:136 only, guarded by `limit := tc.maxRealms; if limit == 0 { limit = defaultMaxRealms }` at 132-135. plan.go:256-259 `if maxRealms > 0 && len(realms) > maxRealms { plan.Dropped = len(realms) - maxRealms; realms = realms[:maxRealms] }`.

## .github/workflows/pr-preview-cleanup.yml:13 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/.github/workflows/pr-preview-cleanup.yml#L13) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: reach
TL;DR: every closed pull request spends a runner and materialises the previews deploy key, almost always to find nothing to delete
Check: `sed -n '12,16p' .github/workflows/pr-preview-cleanup.yml` -- no paths filter; then `gh api 'repos/gnolang/gno/pulls?state=closed&per_page=100' --jq '[.[] | select(.merged_at != null)] | length'` against the count whose files match the pr-preview.yml filter, for the ratio of pointless runs
Details: `pull_request_target: types: [closed]` at 12-14 carries no `paths:` filter, unlike pr-preview.yml, and the job's only gate is `vars.GNOWEB_PREVIEW_BASE_URL != ''` at line 31 — which is set on gnolang/gno itself, so a docs typo or a gnovm change closing also runs the job. It checks out gnolang/gno-previews with PREVIEWS_DEPLOY_KEY persisted on disk (39-44, the `artipacked` suppression acknowledging the persistence) and only then discovers at 56-58 that there is no pr-<N> and exits 0. The finder overstates the clone: fetch-depth defaults to 1, so it is a shallow checkout, but the working tree still materialises every live preview directory, and the standing cost is the credential being present in a job that, on most runs, has nothing to do — any step later added to it inherits that. Fix: give this workflow the same `paths:` filter pr-preview.yml carries, or read the previews repo for pr-<N> over the API before the deploy-key checkout.
Evidence: Read, not run. pr-preview-cleanup.yml:12-14 `on: pull_request_target: types: [closed]` — no paths key. Line 31 `if: vars.GNOWEB_PREVIEW_BASE_URL != ''` is the only guard. Lines 39-44 checkout with `repository: ${{ env.PREVIEWS_REPO }}` and `ssh-key: ${{ secrets.PREVIEWS_DEPLOY_KEY }}`, no persist-credentials: false. Lines 56-58 `if [ ! -d "pr-$PR" ]; then echo "no preview for PR #$PR — nothing to clean"; exit 0; fi` — after the checkout, not before.

## SKIP .github/workflows/pr-preview-cleanup.yml:22 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/.github/workflows/pr-preview-cleanup.yml#L22) · Nit
State: PLAUSIBLE, band: Nit, angle: claims
TL;DR: cleanup and publish share a concurrency group only while the fork still exists, and a deleted fork is a common way a PR closes
Check: gh api repos/gnolang/gno/pulls/<a closed PR whose fork was deleted> --jq '.head.repo' returns null; compare the two concurrency expressions at pr-preview-cleanup.yml:22 and pr-preview-publish.yml:34
Details: The two groups are built from different event payloads: cleanup keys on github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name and head.ref (pr-preview-cleanup.yml:22-24), publish on github.event.workflow_run.head_repository.full_name and head_branch (pr-preview-publish.yml:32-35). They agree while the fork exists. GitHub leaves pull_request.head.repo null once the fork is deleted — which itself auto-closes the PR — and the group then renders as pr-preview-publish--<ref>, not the publish's group, so the close no longer serializes behind an in-flight publish and that publish's replay loop can re-add pr-<N>/ after the cleanup removed it. The preview then survives the closed PR until the previews repo's own daily sweep. Fix: key both workflows on the pull request number, which is never null on either payload.
Evidence: read of both concurrency blocks, quoted above. Attempted to exhibit the null: gh api 'repos/gnolang/gno/pulls?state=closed&per_page=100' --jq '[.[]|select(.head.repo==null)|{n:.number}]' returned [] over the latest 100 closed PRs, so the null-payload case is not reproducible in this environment. Confirming it needs a closed PR whose fork was deleted, or a workflow_run/pull_request_target payload pair captured from a real close.

## .github/workflows/pr-preview.yml:17 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/.github/workflows/pr-preview.yml#L17) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: catalog
TL;DR: after a docs-only or test-only push the preview comment keeps its links and its "rebuilt on every push" line while pointing at an older head
Check: `sed -n '14,27p' .github/workflows/pr-preview.yml` for the filter, then on a pull request that already carries a preview comment push a docs-only commit and confirm no `pr / preview` check runs and the comment body is unchanged
Details: The `paths:` filter at 17-26 gates the whole pipeline, and pr-preview-publish.yml fires only on `workflow_run: workflows: [pr / preview]`, so a push touching none of those paths starts no render, no publish and no comment update. The sticky comment from the last matching push therefore stands unchanged, still closing on comment.go:90, "Rebuilt on every push to this PR; removed when PR #%s closes.", while the reviewer opening its links sees realm pages built from a head the branch no longer has. The trigger is ordinary: any pull request that mixes a realm change with a later docs-only or test-only commit. Fix: put the head sha the snapshot was built from in the comment body, so a stale one is visible without the reviewer reconstructing the push history.
Evidence: Read, not run. pr-preview.yml:14-26 `on: pull_request: types:[opened,synchronize,reopened] paths:` listing examples/gno.land/**, gno.land/pkg/gnoweb/**, gno.land/cmd/gnoweb/**, contribs/gnodev/**, misc/gnopreview/**, .github/workflows/pr-preview.yml. pr-preview-publish.yml:20-23 `on: workflow_run: workflows: [pr / preview] types: [completed]`. comment.go:12 CommentMarker is what makes the comment sticky; comment.go:89-91 writes the closing sentence.

## .github/workflows/pr-preview.yml:49 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/.github/workflows/pr-preview.yml#L49) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: claims
TL;DR: the "after" pane renders the merge commit, so a realm master moved since the merge base shows master's change as the branch's
Check: in the render job, `git rev-parse HEAD` differs from $HEAD_SHA and `git rev-parse HEAD^1` is the base tip; locally, `gh api repos/gnolang/gno/pulls/6194 --jq .merge_commit_sha` then `git log -1 --format=%P <that sha>` shows the two parents. Then render a PR whose base moved on a realm it also touches and compare the before/after pair against `git diff <merge-base> <head> -- <that realm>`
Details: The checkout step at 49-52 passes no `ref:`, so on a `pull_request` event actions/checkout takes the auto-generated `refs/pull/<N>/merge` — head merged into the current base tip — and the render step at 97-102 passes no `-root`, so findRoot resolves to that workspace tree while `-base-root` is the merge-base worktree from line 70. The header comment at 66-69 and renderBase's own comment at main.go:222-228 both assert the opposite, "the pair differs by the realm change alone, not by whatever else moved on master". The blast radius is narrower than the claim reads: the plan's file list (line 64) is merge-base..HEAD_SHA and stays correct, so the drift only shows where master touched a realm the plan already selected or one of its dependencies, and it costs the reviewer a misattributed pixel diff rather than a wrong realm list. Fix: pass `ref: ${{ github.event.pull_request.head.sha }}` on the checkout, which is also what makes the rendered tree match the sha the comment is about.
Evidence: Read, not run. pr-preview.yml:49-52 `- uses: actions/checkout@9c091bb... / with: fetch-depth: 0 / persist-credentials: false` — no `ref:`, which is the documented refs/pull/N/merge default for pull_request. pr-preview.yml:70 `git worktree add --detach "$RUNNER_TEMP/base" "$base"` with base=`git merge-base`. pr-preview.yml:97-102: render is called with -changed/-base-root/-out/-base-url/-pr and no -root, so main.go findRoot walks up from the workspace. main.go:224-228 states the invariant the wiring breaks.

## .github/workflows/pr-preview.yml:78 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/.github/workflows/pr-preview.yml#L78) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: reach
TL;DR: a failing plan step stays green and the job dies minutes later in render, pointing the reader at the wrong step
Check: grep -n 'shell:|defaults:|pipefail|set -e' .github/workflows/pr-preview.yml returns nothing; then bash -e -c 'false | tee x; echo rc=$?' prints rc=0 and continues, and jq -r 'if .gnoweb or (.realms | length > 0) then "yes" else "no" end' on an empty file prints nothing with rc=0
Details: GitHub runs a run: block as bash -e {0}, and pr-preview.yml sets no shell:, no defaults: and no pipefail. In `"$RUNNER_TEMP/gnopreview" plan -changed changed.txt | tee plan.json` (line 78) the pipeline's status is tee's, so any failure of the just-built binary leaves plan.json empty and the step green. jq then reads that empty file, prints nothing and exits 0, so the comparison against "no" fails, skip stays unset, and the job goes on to build gnodev and render before hitting the same error. Fix: add `set -o pipefail` to the step, or a defaults.run.shell: bash block, so the plan failure is the red step.
Evidence: grep -n 'shell:|defaults:|pipefail|set -e' .github/workflows/pr-preview*.yml: no matches. My run: `bash -e -c 'false | tee plan.json; echo rc after pipe=$?; echo reached-next-step'` printed `rc after pipe=0` then `reached-next-step`; the same command under `bash -eo pipefail` exited 1 without printing. jq -r 'if .gnoweb or (.realms | length > 0) then "yes" else "no" end' on the empty plan.json: rc=0, output empty, so the gate prints 'GATE: skip NOT set -> job continues'.

## .gitignore:14 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/.gitignore#L14) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: reach
TL;DR: `make run` leaves misc/gnopreview/_preview.changed in every contributor's git status
Check: cd <head worktree> && git check-ignore -v --no-index misc/gnopreview/_preview.changed misc/gnopreview/_preview/x — the second matches `.gitignore:14:_preview/`, the first matches nothing; `sed -n '6,20p' misc/gnopreview/Makefile` shows `OUT ?= _preview`, `> $(OUT).changed` in `run` and `rm -rf $(OUT) $(OUT).changed` in `clean`
Details: git check-ignore -v --no-index in the head worktree matches misc/gnopreview/_preview/x against .gitignore:14:_preview/ and matches misc/gnopreview/_preview.changed against nothing at all. The tool's own Makefile writes that file in its run target, `git -C $(GNOROOT) diff --name-only $(BASE)... > $(OUT).changed` with OUT ?= _preview, and its clean target removes it beside _preview/, which is the author's own statement that it is a build leftover. It then shows as untracked for the rest of the session and a git add -A commits that developer's changed-file list. Fix: ignore _preview.changed beside _preview/ on .gitignore:14.
Evidence: git check-ignore -v --no-index misc/gnopreview/_preview.changed misc/gnopreview/_preview/x printed one line only, `.gitignore:14:_preview/	misc/gnopreview/_preview/x`; grep -n 'changed|_preview' .gitignore returns only line 14. Makefile lines 6, 14 and 27: `OUT ?= _preview`, `> $(OUT).changed`, `rm -rf $(OUT) $(OUT).changed`.

## misc/gnopreview/Makefile:20 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/Makefile#L20) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: lines
TL;DR: `make run` silently reuses a gnodev built from another branch, so the local preview can render code that is not on screen
Check: run: `touch /tmp/gnodev-probe && GNODEV=/tmp/gnodev-probe make -n run` in misc/gnopreview -- expect a gnodev build in the recipe, observe none
Details: `$(GNODEV)` is a file target at the fixed path /tmp/gnodev (line 4) with no prerequisites, and make considers such a target up to date whenever the file exists, so the `go build -o $(GNODEV)` at line 21 runs exactly once per machine. A contributor who ran `make run` on one branch and then switches to a branch touching contribs/gnodev or gno.land/pkg/gnoweb gets a snapshot rendered by the stale binary with no line of output saying so — the same confusion main.go:222-228 argues the CI pair exists to remove, and CI is unaffected since pr-preview.yml:84-86 builds gnodev itself every run. The fixed /tmp path is also shared, so on a multi-user box the first user's binary is what every later `make run` executes. Fix: build into `$(OUT)/gnodev` or list the gnodev sources as prerequisites, so the binary is rebuilt when the tree it came from moves.
Evidence: Read, and the finder's `GNODEV=/tmp/gnodev-probe make -n run` rechecked against the file. Makefile:13 `run: $(GNODEV)`, Makefile:20-21 `$(GNODEV):` / `cd $(GNOROOT)/contribs/gnodev && go build -o $(GNODEV) .`, Makefile:4 `GNODEV  ?= /tmp/gnodev`. No .PHONY on $(GNODEV) (line 1 lists test run clean install only), so the existing-file rule applies.

Details: comment.go:10-12 documents CommentMarker as what the publishing job matches on, while .github/workflows/pr-preview-publish.yml:169 carries its own literal marker='<!-- gnoweb-pr-preview -->' and nothing ties the two. The only test touching the const, plan_test.go:196, asserts the body contains CommentMarker whatever its value, so changing it compiles and stays green. ## misc/gnopreview/comment.go:170 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/comment.go#L170) · Nit

Check: Index(&Plan{Realms:["gno.land/r/x/leaf"]}, crawler holding only "/r/x/leaf") and grep the output for _t/source/
Details: Index skips a realm whose render page is missing (comment.go:170 `if _, ok := c.pages[u]; !ok { continue }`) and then writes the source and help links on the same row unconditionally (comment.go:173-176). Those two are separate seeds (crawl.go:91-92 `add(u + "$source")`, `add(u + "$help")`), and Run logs and continues on a fetch error, on a non-200 and on a spent per-file budget (crawl.go:130-140), so either can be missing from c.pages while the render page is present. Only captured pages are written, so the reviewer clicks 'source' on the landing page and gets the static host's 404; no test exercises Index at all, the package has none. Fix: guard each tab link on c.pages the way the row itself is guarded, and omit the link when the page was not captured.
Evidence: comment.go:170 guards on the render page alone; comment.go:173-176 emits `<a href="%s/_t/source/">source</a> <a href="%s/_t/help/">help</a>` with no lookup. crawl.go:131-136 `fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "  ! %s: %v\n", u, err)` / `"  ! %s: HTTP %d\n"` then continue. grep -rn 'Index(' misc/gnopreview: one call site (main.go:141), zero tests. The finder's fixture fails at this head: 'index links _t/source/ for a realm whose page was never captured'.

## misc/gnopreview/crawl.go:38 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/crawl.go#L38) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: lines
TL;DR: on a page with no <head>, the noindex meta is planted inside <header> where no crawler reads it
Check: regexp `(?i)<head[^>]*>` against `<header class="b-header">`: expect no match, observe a match on `<header class="b-header">`
Details: setNoindex reaches headRe only when the page carries no robots meta (crawl.go:371-376), which is the case crawl_test.go:175 defends -- a body gnoweb serves without a head. gnoweb's own layout ships `<header class="b-header">` on every page, so on such a body the tag is inserted inside the header element rather than the document head, and the snapshot is indexable in practice while the test still passes, since it only counts the tag. A full page is unaffected: the real <head> precedes <header> and FindStringIndex takes the first match. Fix: `(?i)<head[\s>]`.
Evidence: go1.25.9, TestJudgeHeadReMatchesHeader: headRe matched `<header class="b-header">` in `<header class="b-header">nav</header><p>fragment</p>`; `(?i)<head[\s>]` matched nothing on the same input. Read: crawl.go:374-376 `if loc := headRe.FindStringIndex(body); loc != nil { return body[:loc[1]] + noindexTag + body[loc[1]:] }`; gno.land/pkg/gnoweb/components/layouts/header.html:5 `<header class="b-header">`.

## misc/gnopreview/crawl.go:138 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/crawl.go#L138) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: catalog + reach
TL;DR: per-file source pages are fetched before the budget drops them, so a wide realm renders about twenty pages that are thrown away
Check: Serve a realm page linking 10 distinct $source&file= URLs from an httptest server, run Crawler.Run with FileBudget 2, and count handler invocations: expect 2 captures and 10 fetches. | tests/b9-reach-crawl-caps_test.go, TestB9ReachMaxPagesDoesNotBoundFetches: httptest server counting requests, one $source page with 3000 file links, MaxPages=10, FileBudget=2; expect the cap to bound the requests, observe 3004
Details: Run() enqueues a link when inScope says yes (crawl.go:148), and inScope asks wantFile, whose budget branch is `c.fileBudget[realm] < c.FileBudget` (crawl.go:231) — a counter only chargeFile (crawl.go:236) ever increments, and chargeFile runs at crawl.go:138, after getRetry has already returned the body. Every per-file link on a $source page is therefore fetched and only then discarded. The finder's failure scenario overstates the reach twice over. fileBudget() at main.go:217-221 returns 0 unless plan.Gnoweb, so on any PR that does not touch gnoweb no per-file page is in scope at all (`0 < 0` is false); and a realm with changed files takes wantFile's exact ChangedFiles branch, which chargeFile short-circuits at crawl.go:250 — so it is not 'every realm', and it is not fork-controlled, because any file a fork adds is a changed file and lands in ChangedFiles. What a user actually loses is CI seconds on gnoweb PRs: the four seed realms are crawled with FileBudget 2, and gno.land/r/gnoland/boards2/v0 has 22 .gno files and gno.land/r/gnoland/blog 5, so about 23 chroma-highlighted source renders are fetched and dropped per crawl, and the tool crawls head and base. README.md:118's '-max-pages cap (400) backstops the rest' is about captured pages and is not falsified by this. Fix: charge the budget in inScope, at enqueue time, and let chargeFile only refund a link that 404s.
Evidence: Ran the finder's fixture from the file in my own detached worktree at ecf7af0 with go1.25.9: `go test -count=1 -v -run TestB9ReachMaxPagesDoesNotBoundFetches .` logged `B9REACH captured=6 served=3004 maxPages=10 fileBudget=2`, PASS, Run() returning nil — 3004 requests served to keep 6 pages, with MaxPages=10 never consulted because crawl.go:126 tests `len(c.pages)`. The narrowing is read and measured at the same head: main.go:217-221 `func fileBudget(plan *Plan) int { if plan.Gnoweb { return GnowebFileBudget }; return 0 }`; gnowebSeedRealms at plan.go:37-42 is home, docs/security_patterns, gnoland/boards2/v0, gnoland/blog; `ls examples/gno.land/r/gnoland/boards2/v0/*.gno | wc -l` = 22 and gnoland/blog = 5. crawl.go:210-214 documents the split deliberately ('Charging it is chargeFile's job ... a link that is discovered and then 404s must not spend a slot'), which is why this is polish and not a blocker. No merge-base run: misc/gnopreview does not exist at bbd9b2ffe.
Artifact: reviews/pr/6xxx/6194-gnoweb-pr-preview/1-ecf7af0/tests/b9-reach-crawl-caps_test.go

## SKIP misc/gnopreview/crawl.go:243 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/crawl.go#L243) · Nit

TL;DR: a per-file page with an empty name would be kept without spending a budget slot, but nothing gnoweb serves links one
Check: call c.inScope and c.chargeFile on '/r/x/y$source&file=' with FileBudget=2 and no ChangedFiles; expect a charge, observe true from chargeFile with fileBudget untouched
Details: The asymmetry is real and read-settled: wantFile takes the budget branch for an empty name (crawl.go:227-232) and chargeFile returns true before charging when name == "" (crawl.go:243-245), so inScope admits the URL and the capture costs nothing. It has no producer in the crawled surface, though: every directory href gets a file name appended at view_directory.go:45, directory.html:48 hardcodes README.md, and the overview's license links interpolate a FileName that overview_license.go only ever sets from a matched file name. Reaching it needs a realm's own Render output to emit such an href. Fix: treat an empty file value as a per-file page in chargeFile so the two functions agree.
Evidence: crawl.go:243 `if name == "" { return true // not a per-file page }`; crawl.go:231 `return c.fileBudget[realm] < c.FileBudget`. ## misc/gnopreview/crawl.go:361 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/crawl.go#L361) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: lines
TL;DR: a rewritten asset path that needs quoting comes back as an invalid CSS url token
Check: read crawl.go:41 and 359-362: the regex captures the quote as optional and the replacement never re-emits it
Details: cssRe matches an optional quote and the replacement always emits a bare url(...), so quoting the source had is lost; a path carrying a space or a paren then yields a token the browser rejects and the declaration is dropped with no error. Reachability at this head is narrow: the shipped gnoweb stylesheets write relative refs (`url(fonts/intervar/Intervar.woff2)`) that cssRe never matches, and no file under gno.land/pkg/gnoweb/public carries a space or a paren, so the bite needs an inline /public/ url() plus such a name. Fix: emit `"url(\"" + up + path + "\")"`.
Evidence: go1.25.9, TestJudgeCSSReDropsQuotes: in `background:url("/public/fonts/My Font.woff2")` -> out `background:url(../public/fonts/My Font.woff2)`. Read: crawl.go:41 `url\(\s*"?(/public/[^)"']*)"?\s*\)`; crawl.go:361 `return "url(" + up + strings.TrimPrefix(sub[1], "/") + ")"`. grep of gno.land/pkg/gnoweb/public/*.css: three url() refs, all relative; find over that tree for a name with a space or paren: no hit.

## misc/gnopreview/crawl.go:490 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/crawl.go#L490) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: claims
TL;DR: the listing branch of urlToFile is dead in the binary, so a test row promises snapshots of gnoweb listings that are never taken
Check: go test -run TestListingBranchNeverRuns: a fake gnoweb linking "/r/x/y/" on every page, then assert no captured URL ends in "/" and no File contains "/_dir/" | cd misc/gnopreview && go test -run TestProbeListingBranchUnreachable -v . with the probe file: seeds carry no trailing slash and inScope is false for every trailing-slash form
Details: No URL the crawler captures can end in a slash. Seeds builds every entry from urlOf and path.Dir (crawl.go:85-99) and its directory walk stops while strings.Count(d, "/") >= 2, so it never emits "/" or a trailing slash; inScope returns false for any base ending in "/" (crawl.go:191-193), and Run only enqueues a link that is a seed or in scope. So urlToFile's listing branch never fires outside its own test, and TestURLToFile's {"/r/", "r/_dir/index.html"} row is the only thing that exercises it. A reader who trusts that row will assume gnoweb's directory listings are in the snapshot; they are left to the live site. Fix: drop the branch and its row, and say in the Seeds comment that listings go to the live site.
Evidence: read of crawl.go:73-105 (Seeds), 105-150 (Run's queue admission), 186-196 (inScope's trailing-slash reject) and 487-494 (the listing flag). crawl.go:191-193: // "/r/x/y/" is gnoweb's listing view ... if strings.HasSuffix(base, "/") { return false }. crawl_test.go:83 pins {"/r/gnoland/home/", false}.

## misc/gnopreview/crawl.go:496 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/crawl.go#L496) · Nit

Check: go test -run TestReachDirListingBranchIsDead -v . : assert inScope("/r/gnoland/") and that no Seeds() entry ends in "/"
Details: The listing flag is strings.HasSuffix(base, "/") && base != "/" (crawl.go:490), and no crawled URL ends in a slash: Seeds builds from urlOf and path.Dir (crawl.go:85-99) and inScope rejects any trailing-slash base (crawl.go:191-193), so Run's queue never holds one. TestSplitURL's {"/r/x/y/", ...} row, TestCanonicalURL's {"/r/x/y/", "/r/x/y/"} row and TestURLToFile's {"/r/", "r/_dir/index.html"} row therefore guard a shape the binary cannot reach. They read as coverage of the directory-listing path while the real listing link is the one silently going offsite. Fix: delete the branch and those rows, keeping TestCrawlerInScope's {"/r/gnoland/home/", false} as the statement that listings are left to the live site.
Evidence: read of crawl.go:485-505, 73-105 and 186-196, with crawl_test.go:16, 36, 63 and 83 as the pinned rows. The finder's named tests TestURLToFileIsSafe and TestSplitURLKeepsTrailingSlash do not exist under those names in crawl_test.go; the rows above are what pin the shape.

## misc/gnopreview/crawl.go:599 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/crawl.go#L599) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: lines
TL;DR: a gnodev that exits cleanly reports a format-verb error where the reason should be
Check: fmt.Errorf("x: %w", error(nil)) in a scratch main: expect a usable message, observe "x: %!w(<nil>)"
Details: startGnodev sends cmd.Wait() down the died channel (main.go:202), and Wait returns nil on exit status 0 -- a flag gnodev rejects cleanly, a subcommand that prints usage and returns. waitReady wraps that nil with %w, and Go renders it as the verb error. The channel was added so a gnodev that dies on startup says why; the clean-exit case is the one where it says nothing usable. Fix: branch on a nil err and report the clean exit, or wrap with %v.
Evidence: go1.25.9, TestJudgeWrapNilExit: `gnodev exited before serving /r/x/y — see the gnodev log: %!w(<nil>)`. Read: main.go:202 `died <- cmd.Wait()`; crawl.go:599 `return fmt.Errorf("gnodev exited before serving %s — see the gnodev log: %w", probe, err)`.

Details: The header at crawl_test.go:51-52 reads "No output path may contain $, : or & — they survive a URL but not every static host", and the table under it compares seven exact strings, none of which carries one of those characters in the base. urlToFile slugs args and query and passes base through untouched (crawl.go:487-493), so urlToFile("/r/x&y") returns "r/x&y/index.html". Nothing reaches it today because inScope requires base to equal a selected realm's path, so what is lost is a documented invariant no test holds, not a live bug. Fix: assert it over the table — strings.ContainsAny(got, "$:&") must be false for every row — instead of stating it in prose.
Evidence: read of crawl_test.go:49-68 and crawl.go:480-494. splitURL cuts on "$" and ":" only (crawl.go:276-280), so "&" stays in base; base = strings.Trim(base, "/") is appended to parts with no slug() call, while args and query both go through slug().

## misc/gnopreview/main.go:96 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/main.go#L96) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: lines
TL;DR: a mistyped subcommand blocks on stdin and walks every examples package before saying the command does not exist
Check: go run . redner in misc/gnopreview with stdin a terminal: expect the usage error, observe the process blocked on the stdin read
Details: run() calls readLines at main.go:87 and BuildPlan at main.go:91, and only then reaches the switch at :96 that decides whether the command exists. -changed defaults to "-" (main.go:66) and readLines("-") reads /dev/stdin (main.go:309-310), so `gnopreview redner` from a terminal hangs with no output; fed an EOF it walks examples/gno.land through LoadPkgs before printing unknown command "redner". The user loses a typo's worth of time to a hang whose cause the program already knows. Fix: switch on cmd immediately after fs.Parse and let each command do its own reading.
Evidence: read of main.go:56-103 and main.go:306-324. The ordering is literal: changed, err := readLines(cfg.changed) at :87, plan, err := BuildPlan(...) at :91, switch cmd at :96, return fmt.Errorf("unknown command %q", cmd) at :102.

## misc/gnopreview/main.go:106 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/main.go#L106) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: claims
TL;DR: a second local render serves the first run's pages, linked from nothing
Check: run render twice into the same -out with a smaller -max-realms the second time, then diff `find _preview -name '*.html'` against the realms in _preview/preview.json
Details: render only os.MkdirAll's cfg.out at main.go:106 and never clears it. Crawler.Write writes what it captured and Index(plan, c) lists only c.pages (comment.go:170), so a rerun after the plan shrinks leaves every page of the earlier plan on disk, served by the snapshot and listed by neither index.html nor preview.json. The README's own local flow at README.md:51-56 renders into _preview and serves that directory, which is exactly the rerun. CI hands each job a fresh checkout, so the cost is a local user reading a snapshot that disagrees with its own index rather than a bad publish. Fix: os.RemoveAll(cfg.out) before the MkdirAll, or refuse a non-empty -out without a flag.

## misc/gnopreview/main.go:176 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/main.go#L176) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: lines
TL;DR: -port above 55535 derives an out-of-range RPC port and fails naming a number the operator never typed
Check: gnopreview render -port 56000 with a built gnodev: expect a flag error, observe a bind failure on 66000
Details: startGnodev builds the node listener as tcp://127.0.0.1:%d with port+10000 (main.go:176) and nothing validates cfg.port (main.go:69). -port 56000 asks gnodev to bind 66000, outside the u16 range, so the process exits, died fires and waitReady returns "gnodev exited before serving" (crawl.go:598-599) pointing at a log about port 66000. The base pass at cfg.port+1 (main.go:251) fails the same way. The cost is a confusing failure on a flag the tool could have rejected. Fix: bound cfg.port to 1..55534 right after fs.Parse and name both derived ports in the error.
Evidence: read of main.go:167-179 and main.go:56-95. Line 176: "-node-rpc-listener", fmt.Sprintf("tcp://127.0.0.1:%d", port+10000) — 56000+10000 = 66000 > 65535, and no check stands between fs.Parse at :77 and the call at :117.

## misc/gnopreview/main.go:177 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/main.go#L177) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: lines
TL;DR: the per-port gnodev home never lands under -out, so the workflow's cleanup of it deletes nothing
Check: gnopreview render -port 56000 with a built gnodev: expect a flag error, observe a bind failure on 66000
Details: cfg.out is the -out flag, default "_preview", and is never made absolute -- filepath.Abs is applied to -root alone (main.go:288). So -home is handed to gnodev as a relative path, and execLocalApp chdirs into -C examples before anything reads it, resolving the home under <root>/examples. setup_address_book.go answers a missing home with a warning and never creates it, so the directory the comment names is never made: pr-preview.yml:103's `rm -rf _preview/.gnodev-*` removes nothing, and if gnodev ever did create it, it would land inside the examples checkout. The collision the comment guards against does not occur either way, since the two passes name different ports. Fix: resolve cfg.out with filepath.Abs once at startup. Candidate anchored main.go:186; the -home argument is main.go:177.
Evidence: read, head worktree: main.go:67 `fs.StringVar(&cfg.out, "out", "_preview", "output directory")` and no Abs on it (main.go:288's filepath.Abs covers -root); main.go:177 `"-home", filepath.Join(cfg.out, fmt.Sprintf(".gnodev-%d", port))` beside main.go:178 `"-C", examples`; contribs/gnodev/command_local.go:87-91 `if cfg.chdir != "" { if err := os.Chdir(cfg.chdir); err != nil {...} }`; contribs/gnodev/setup_address_book.go:18 `} else if !osm.DirExists(cfg.home) {` -> warn, no MkdirAll; .github/workflows/pr-preview.yml:103 `rm -rf _preview/gnodev.log _preview/gnodev-base.log _preview/.gnodev-*`.

## misc/gnopreview/main.go:251 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/main.go#L251) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: reach
TL;DR: -port 0 is accepted and costs the full 5-minute timeout before reporting a message that names no port
Check: gnopreview render -port 0 on any plan: expect a clear error, observe a 5 minute wait then "gnoweb not ready"
Details: Nothing between fs.Parse and use range-checks cfg.port (main.go:69 declares it, main.go in full carries no validation). With -port 0 gnodev is told -web-listener 127.0.0.1:0, binds a random port and stays alive, while main.go:124 builds Base as http://127.0.0.1:0 and waitReady dials that. The died channel only carries a value when gnodev exits (main.go:199-204), so the select at crawl.go:597-601 never takes it and the loop sleeps a second at a time to the deadline. The operator waits the full -timeout and gets "gnoweb not ready after 5m0s", which names neither the flag nor the port it produced. Fix: listen on :0 in-process, read the bound port back, and reject 0 at flag time.
Evidence: read of main.go:69 (flag, no check), main.go:124 (Base built straight from cfg.port), main.go:199-204 (died fires only on cmd.Wait) and crawl.go:589-610 (waitReady loops to the deadline, returning "gnoweb not ready after %s"). Triggering input: gnopreview render -port 0.

## misc/gnopreview/plan.go:117 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/plan.go#L117) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: refactor
TL;DR: examples/gno.land is enumerated end to end twice on every plan build
Check: add `var zzDirs, zzEntries int`, `zzEntries++` at the top of the WalkDir callback and `zzDirs++` before os.ReadDir(p), then a test calling LoadPkgs("../..") printing both counters (recipe in the artifact header)
Details: The finder's artifact ships no such test, so I built it: the counter patch its header describes, plus a test calling LoadPkgs("../.."). filepath.WalkDir reads all 304 directories under examples/gno.land to produce the 1530 entries it hands the callback; the callback acts only on d.IsDir(), discards those entries, and issues os.ReadDir(p) on the very directory the walk just read, 304 times. Handling entries as the walk delivers them (gnomod.toml into one set, non-test .gno files keyed by filepath.Dir(p)) removes the second pass and the hasMod/ents bookkeeping with it. What it costs the user is a doubled directory scan in a CI job that then boots gnodev and screenshots, so milliseconds, not seconds. Fix: drop the os.ReadDir(p) call and accumulate per-directory state from the callback's own d as the walk delivers files.
Evidence: head, scratch worktree at ecf7af0, counter patch applied, `go test -run TestZZCountsDirectoryReads -v .`: `packages=145 walk-callback-entries=1531 callback ReadDir calls=304`, PASS. Independent cross-check with no patch, os.walk over examples/gno.land: `directories (incl. base) = 304  total entries = 1530  callback invocations = 1+entries = 1531` — the 304 ReadDir calls re-read exactly what the walk had already read. plan.go restored to 345 lines after the run. Merge base has no misc/gnopreview.
Artifact: reviews/pr/6xxx/6194-gnoweb-pr-preview/1-ecf7af0/tests/judge-231-plan-double-enumeration_test.go

## misc/gnopreview/plan.go:142 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/plan.go#L142) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: claims + reach + lines + catalog
TL;DR: Pkg.Draft is parsed for every package on every run and read nowhere, so draft realms are previewed like any other
Check: grep -n 'Draft' misc/gnopreview/*.go: expect a read site, observe only the declaration (plan.go:58) and the assignment (plan.go:142) | grep -n 'Draft' misc/gnopreview/*.go — two hits, both writes | grep -rn '\.Draft\b' misc/gnopreview: expect a use, observe only plan.go:58 and plan.go:142 | grep -rn 'Draft' misc/gnopreview --include='*.go' returns only plan.go:58 (the field) and plan.go:142 (the assignment) | grep -rn 'Draft' misc/gnopreview/ -- five hits: plan.go:58 decl, plan.go:142 assign, and nothing else; no read site anywhere in the module | grep -n 'Draft' misc/gnopreview/*.go: expect a read site, observe only plan.go:58 and plan.go:142 | grep -rn '\.Draft\b' --include='*.go' . | grep gnopreview — one hit, the assignment at plan.go:142
Details: grep -rn 'Draft' over misc/gnopreview returns exactly two lines, the field declaration at plan.go:58 and the assignment at plan.go:142; nothing reads it and Pkg carries no json tags, so it is not serialized out either. modFlags' doc at plan.go:153 calls them the two booleans we care about, while only Ignore reaches a filter, at plan.go:220 and plan.go:241. Either the field and half of modFlags are dead code, or draft packages were meant to be excluded from the preview and are not. Fix: decide which, then drop Draft with its parse branch or filter on it beside Ignore.
Evidence: grep -rn 'Draft' /…/misc/gnopreview/ → plan.go:58 `Draft   bool     // gnomod.toml: draft = true` and plan.go:142 `pkg.Draft, pkg.Ignore = modFlags(...)`. No third hit, tests included.

## SKIP misc/gnopreview/plan.go:156 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/plan.go#L156) · Nit
State: PLAUSIBLE, band: Nit, angle: catalog
TL;DR: an unreadable gnomod.toml would be read as a live package, but nothing in a CI checkout makes one unreadable
Check: chmod 000 examples/gno.land/r/x/<pkg>/gnomod.toml as a non-root user, run BuildPlan on that root, observe the package present in plan.Realms
Details: modFlags collapses any ReadFile error into (false, false) at plan.go:156-158, so a package whose gnomod.toml cannot be read is treated as neither draft nor ignored. LoadPkgs calls it only after ReadDir reported a gnomod.toml entry in that directory (plan.go:125, 142), so the error needs a permission change, a removal between the two calls, or a directory named gnomod.toml — none of which a fresh CI checkout produces. The mechanism is real; the loss it claims, an ignore = true package rendered and linked, has no reproducer here. Fix: return the error from modFlags and let LoadPkgs fail loudly rather than guess.
Evidence: plan.go:155-158 `func modFlags(p string) (draft, ignore bool) { b, err := os.ReadFile(p); if err != nil { return false, false }`. Confirming it would need the finder's chmod 000 run on a real examples/gno.land/r/x/<pkg>/gnomod.toml with BuildPlan observed to keep the package.

## misc/gnopreview/plan.go:157 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/plan.go#L157) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: catalog
TL;DR: an unreadable gnomod.toml silently previews a package that asked to be ignored
Check: chmod 000 on an ignore = true gnomod.toml and run BuildPlan: expect the package excluded, observe it in plan.Realms
Details: LoadPkgs sets hasMod from the directory listing at plan.go:125, so modFlags runs only on a file already known to exist, and plan.go:156-158 turns every read error into false, false with nothing on stderr. An ignore = true package whose file cannot be read — permissions, a dangling symlink, a truncated checkout — then passes the Ignore filters at plan.go:220 and plan.go:241 and is booted into gnodev and rendered, the opposite of what the file asked for. The trigger needs a damaged tree, so this is latent rather than something the workflow will hit. Fix: return the error from modFlags, or at least print it, instead of reporting the package as neither draft nor ignored.
Evidence: read plan.go:155-159: `b, err := os.ReadFile(p); if err != nil { return false, false }`, reached from plan.go:142 only under the `hasMod` branch established at plan.go:125-133 (`if !hasMod || len(files) == 0 { return nil }`). The two consumers are plan.go:220 `if p := byDir[path.Dir(f)]; p != nil && !p.Ignore` and plan.go:241 `if !pkgs[p].Realm || pkgs[p].Ignore { continue }`.

## misc/gnopreview/plan.go:212 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/plan.go#L212) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: refactor + lines
TL;DR: BuildPlan spells the gnowebPaths prefix test by hand while calling hasPrefixAny for the same test 32 lines later
Check: apply the substitution, then cd misc/gnopreview && go vet ./... && go test ./... | built the head tool and ran it against the head worktree: echo gno.land/pkg/gnoweb/app_test.go | gnopreview plan -root <head> -changed - | git apply reviews/pr/6xxx/6194-gnoweb-pr-preview/1-ecf7af0/tests/b7-refactor-hasprefixany.patch at ecf7af0, then cd misc/gnopreview && gofmt -l . && go vet ./... && go test ./...
Details: plan.go:212-216 loops over gnowebPaths setting plan.Gnoweb = true on a strings.HasPrefix match, with no break, so it walks all three prefixes even after matching. hasPrefixAny at plan.go:326-334 is that predicate, and BuildPlan already calls it at plan.go:244 for indirectExclude, so the same function uses both spellings. Output is identical either way: both are true exactly when some prefix matches. What the maintainer loses is that a change to prefix matching, case folding or a trailing-slash rule, has to land in two places, and the open-coded loop is the one a reader forgets.
Evidence: Read at ecf7af0: plan.go:212 `for _, gw := range gnowebPaths {` ... `if strings.HasPrefix(f, gw) { plan.Gnoweb = true }` against plan.go:326 `func hasPrefixAny(s string, prefixes []string) bool` and its existing caller plan.go:244 `if !changedPkgs[p] && hasPrefixAny(p, indirectExclude)`. Finder's patch applied in my scratch worktree: git diff --numstat = `2	4	misc/gnopreview/plan.go` (4 lines -> 2), gofmt -l . silent, go vet ./... silent, `ok  github.com/gnolang/gno/misc/gnopreview	0.004s`; unpatched baseline at the same head the same ok line. Merge base bbd9b2f has no misc/gnopreview.
Artifact: reviews/pr/6xxx/6194-gnoweb-pr-preview/1-ecf7af0/tests/b7-refactor-hasprefixany.patch

## misc/gnopreview/plan.go:220 [gh](https://github.com/gnolang/gno/blob/ecf7af0f29abe4737a52803d672bc5a33c17cc60/misc/gnopreview/plan.go#L220) · Nit
State: CONFIRMED, band: Nit, angle: refactor
TL;DR: a realm the pull request deletes leaves no trace in the plan or in the comment
Check: apply edit list B from the artifact header, then cd misc/gnopreview && go test ./...
Details: LoadPkgs walks the head tree (plan.go:107-110), so a realm the branch removed has no entry in byDir. BuildPlan's only branch for a changed path is `if p := byDir[path.Dir(f)]; p != nil && !p.Ignore` (plan.go:220), and the nil lookup falls through recording nothing. The realm is then absent from ChangedRealms, from Realms and from Dropped, which counts cap losses alone (plan.go:256-258), so a pull request whose whole effect is that a realm stopped existing produces no comment at all, or one that never mentions it. Fix: record a changed path whose package is missing at head and name it in the comment as removed.
Evidence: plan.go:109 `base := filepath.Join(root, filepath.FromSlash(pkgRoot))` with root the head checkout; plan.go:220 is the only branch consuming a changed path, and it has no else; plan.go:257 `plan.Dropped = len(realms) - maxRealms` is the cap alone.
